Appendix C — Complete revset operator/function reference

Expression/functionResult and signatureOperational note
all(), none()Universe or empty set.Use deliberately; all() can include more history than a normal visible log.
root()Root commit singleton.Graph anchor; not a normal mutable working change.
parents(X,[depth])Parents to optional depth.Without depth, the documented traversal form can walk ancestry; use postfix X- for immediate parents.
children(X,[depth])Children to optional depth.Forks can produce several results; commands requiring one need a boundary.
ancestors(X,[depth])Ancestor closure.Includes selected X according to the function's closure semantics.
descendants(X,[depth])Descendant closure.Can include multiple branches and hidden/visible members depending on domain.
first_parent(X,[depth])First-parent traversal.Merge parent order is a policy choice; do not use for generic reachability.
first_ancestors(X,[depth])First-parent ancestry.Useful for release-line reports, not for all changes reachable from a merge.
reachable(S,D)Members of domain D reachable from sources S.Source and domain are separate; this is safer than a broad closure in a multi-root repo.
connected(X)Connected portion of a set.Useful before commands whose patch operation rejects gaps.
heads(X), roots(X)Maximal/minimal members of X.Reduce a graph set to publication tips or stack roots.
latest(X,[count])Most recent selected commits.Ordering is jj's graph/date policy; inspect result before destructive use.
fork_point(X)Graph-derived fork point.May be non-singleton or absent in complex/disconnected inputs; test cardinality.
merge_point(X)Graph-derived merge point.Use to reason about common integration rather than a bookmark name.
bisect(X)Graph bisection point.Complements jj bisect run; test semantics against a known range.
exactly(X,N)Filters/validates X to exact cardinality.Useful in aliases that must fail closed before edit or describe.
coalesce(X,...)First non-empty commit set.Useful for fallback trunk names; document precedence.
merges(), forks()Merge/fork predicates.Graph shape, not ref state.
bookmarks([PATTERN])Commits targeted by local bookmarks.Pattern filters names; use remote form for imported refs.
remote_bookmarks([NAME],[REMOTE])Commits targeted by remote bookmarks.Remote qualification is essential with GitHub/Gerrit together.
tracked_remote_bookmarks(...), untracked_remote_bookmarks(...)Remote refs grouped by tracking relation.Tracking is local relationship metadata, not automatic @ following.
tags([PATTERN]), remote_tags(...)Tag targets.0.45.1 adds tracked/untracked remote-tag predicates.
git_refs()Git ref targets.Backend/ref view; not a substitute for bookmark policy.
visible_heads()Visible graph heads.Repository-level display boundary.
change_id(PREFIX), commit_id(PREFIX)Identity-based selection.Ambiguous prefixes require disambiguation; commit and change identity differ.
description(PATTERN), subject(PATTERN)Description/first-line predicates.Use explicit exact, substring, glob, or regex.
author(PATTERN), name/email formsAuthor metadata predicates.Author is not committer; email matching is usually the robust policy.
committer(PATTERN), name/email formsCommitter metadata predicates.Useful for imported/re-written provenance reports.
author_date(PATTERN), committer_date(PATTERN)Date predicates.Use explicit timezone/cutoff policy and test boundaries.
mine()Author email matches current user.Not a server identity or committer predicate.
signed()Signed commits.Presence differs from cryptographic status verification.
files(EXPR)Commits whose trees match a fileset expression.Nested fileset language is independent of revset syntax.
diff_lines(TEXT,[FILES])Commits whose diff contains matching lines.Added/removed variants distinguish direction; quote patterns.
conflicts(), empty()Stored conflict/empty predicates.conflicts() is the revset name; template uses conflict().
divergent(), present(X)Divergent changes; present commit resolution.Useful for forensic checks and ambiguous identities.
mutable(), immutable()Mutability predicates.Computed relative to immutable-head configuration, not simply local/remote naming.
visible(), hidden()Visibility predicates.Hidden is not erased; avoid GC during recovery.
working_copies(), at_operation(OP,X)Workspace and historical operation predicates.Local workspace/operation state; not transferable through Git.
trunk()Configured trunk commit/set.Configuration-dependent; inspect effective config before automation.
builtin_log(), builtin_immutable_heads()Built-in configured expressions.Useful as a base when customising defaults; release-sensitive.

Operator precedence card

f(x)
x-  x+
p:x
x::  ::x  x::y     x..  ..x  x..y
~x
x & y    x ~ y
x | y

Function calls bind strongest and union weakest. x..y means ::y ~ ::x; x::y means x:: & ::y. Parenthesise any expression whose intended domain is not obvious. A revset is a set value: use log --count to inspect cardinality and roots/heads to make graph boundaries explicit.

Revset functions by family

This table is a reader-facing semantic index for the functions extracted from the 0.44 help. Optional arguments and pattern forms are abbreviated where the command’s help gives several overloads; the teaching sections provide evaluated examples. The 0.45.x-only tag predicates are explicitly marked.

FamilyFunction/signatureResult and useful composition
Universeall(), none()All commits in the current view, or the empty set. Use as algebraic identities; broad all() may include more history than an ordinary log.
Anchorroot()The repository root singleton. Use as a graph anchor, not as a working change.
Boundaryheads(X), roots(X)Reduce X to nodes with no selected children or parents. Applying them before/after an intersection changes the result.
Visibilityvisible_heads(), visible(), hidden()Visibility/presentation boundaries. Hidden does not mean unreachable or deleted.
Immutabilityimmutable_heads(), builtin_immutable_heads()Configured or builtin rewrite-protection boundaries. Compare them when diagnosing a policy change.
Traversalancestors(X), descendants(X)Transitive parent/child closure including the input. Intersect to form corridors.
Traversalparents(X), children(X)Parent/child relationships, with optional depth forms where documented. Use for explicit edge inspection.
Traversalfirst_parent(X), first_ancestors(X)Follow first-parent integration history rather than every reachable parent.
Traversalreachable(X,Y), connected(X)Reachability/connectivity predicates for non-linear graphs. Test direction; reachability is not symmetric.
Topologymerges(X), forks(X)Select merge/fork topology. A merge is not merely a commit with two reviews.
Topologylatest(X, N)Choose latest commits by timestamp/ordering from X. A policy choice, not a cardinality assertion.
Topologyfork_point(X,Y), merge_point(X,Y)Compute common graph points. Ambiguity and multiple common ancestors require inspection.
Statemutable(), immutable()Configured mutability predicates. Intersect with reachability for safe work selections.
Stateempty()Commits whose tree has no change relative to its parents. Description alone does not make a tree non-empty.
Stateconflicts()Commits with files in a conflicted state. This is distinct from a merge commit and from conflict markers currently visible on disk.
Statedivergent()Multiple visible evolutions of one change ID. Treat as a logical-identity problem, not just a graph fork.
Identitycommit_id(PATTERN)Select commit IDs matching a pattern. Exact immutable-object selection.
Identitychange_id(PATTERN)Select logical change IDs/evolutions. More stable across ordinary rewrite than commit ID.
Identitypresent(X)Resolve only commits present in the current view; useful for optional symbols in aliases.
Authorshipauthor(PATTERN), author_name(PATTERN), author_email(PATTERN)Match author signature fields. Combine with date/topology and explicit pattern kinds.
Authorshipauthor_date(PATTERN)Match author timestamps. Author time can differ from committer time after imports/rebases.
Committercommitter(PATTERN), committer_name(PATTERN), committer_email(PATTERN)Match committer metadata. Useful for identifying the process that rewrote a commit.
Committercommitter_date(PATTERN)Match commit/rewrite time. Do not substitute it for author date in provenance queries.
Descriptiondescription(PATTERN), subject(PATTERN)Match full description or first-line subject. Newlines, trailers, and case matter.
Refsbookmarks(), remote_bookmarks()Commits carrying local or remote bookmark refs. Use unions for “published anywhere”.
Refstracked_remote_bookmarks(), untracked_remote_bookmarks()Partition remote bookmarks by tracking relationship; useful for publication audits.
Refstags(), remote_tags()Tag-bearing commits, local/remote. Tags are release names, not review branches.
Refstracked_remote_tags(), untracked_remote_tags()0.45.x additions. In 0.45.x untracked remote tags participate in default immutable heads.
Refsgit_refs()Git ref-derived selection. Backend/import state determines what is visible.
Workspaceworkspaces(), working_copies()Commits associated with workspace records/current working copies. @ remains workspace-local.
Historyat_operation(OP, X)Evaluate X in a historical operation view. Do not mix its graph with current filesystem state casually.
Historyevolution(X)Select change evolutions/predecessors/successors as documented. Use with evolog for forensic inspection.
Filesfile(PATTERN), files(PATTERN)Match commits by changed files or tree paths. Pattern scope and diff direction are significant.
Filesdiff_lines(TEXT, [FILESET])Match changes whose diff lines contain the text, optionally limited by a fileset. Beware generated/binary content.
Filesdiff_lines_added(TEXT, [FILESET]), diff_lines_removed(TEXT, [FILESET])Match added/removed diff lines. Use bounded ranges and inspect false positives.
Reviewmine()Author matches configured user identity. A convenient predicate, not proof of ownership.
Reviewsigned()Select signed commits where supported. Cryptographic validity and server trust remain separate checks.
Query controlcoalesce(X,Y,...)Return the first present/non-empty set/value. Useful in aliases with optional refs.
Query controlexactly(X,N)Evaluate X and error unless it has exactly N members; useful before singleton commands.
Refstrunk()Resolve the configured default trunk bookmark. Prefer an explicit remote bookmark when a script must be unambiguous.
Query controlbuiltin_log()The builtin log revset/default. Use as a base when preserving release defaults.
Bisectbisect()Expose bisection state/candidate selection. Candidate semantics belong to jj bisect run.
Aliasesconfigured aliasesRevset aliases are functions in the same language. They can take parameters and can shadow names; version them and test them.

The table intentionally says “as named by target help” for the conflict spelling because generated inventories can preserve aliases and version-specific terms differently. Run the 0.44 capture and supplied validation fixture when porting a query; do not silently turn an unavailable predicate into a prose synonym.

Appendix D — Complete fileset reference

FormMeaningExample
literal/prefixcwd-relative path prefix'src'
cwd:"path"explicit cwd-relative prefix'cwd:"src/lib"'
file:"path"exact cwd-relative file'file:"Cargo.toml"'
glob:"pattern"cwd-relative wildcard'glob:"*.rs"'
prefix-glob:"pattern"wildcard plus recursive prefix'prefix-glob:"src/*.rs"'
root:"path"root-relative prefix'root:"src"'
root-file:"path"exact root-relative file'root-file:"Cargo.toml"'
root-glob:"pattern"root-relative wildcard'root-glob:"**/*.rs"'
root-prefix-glob:"pattern"root-relative wildcard plus prefix'root-prefix-glob:"src/**"'
glob-i:case-insensitive glob variant'glob-i:"*.TXT"'
all()all paths'all() ~ glob:"**/*.lock"'
none()no pathsUseful as an alias fallback.

Fileset precedence is function, pattern, complement, intersection/difference, union. The language supports &, ~, |, and parentheses. Shell quoting is mandatory around operators and recommended around every complex expression.

Standalone reference cards

Appendix D — Fileset grammar and consumer matrix

The target fileset language has a small grammar with explicit scope prefixes and set operators. The shell passes one quoted expression to jj; jj then parses the expression. The conceptual grammar is:

fileset  := atom | fileset " | " fileset | fileset " & " fileset
          | fileset " ~ " fileset | "~" fileset | "(" fileset ")"
atom     := "all()" | "none()" | prefix ":" pattern
prefix   := cwd | file | glob | prefix-glob | root | root-file
          | root-glob | root-prefix-glob
          | glob-i | cwd-glob-i | root-glob-i | ...

The exact case-insensitive and cwd-prefixed variants are captured in the 0.44 help. The important design rule is not to infer a prefix’s scope from the command’s current directory. Use root-file: and root-glob: in repository scripts; use cwd-file: when a command is intentionally relative to the caller.

FormBase directoryMatching kindUse when
file:PCommand/working-copy path contextOne literal pathYou want exactly one named file.
glob:PCommand/working-copy path contextGlob patternYou need a pattern and have controlled cwd.
prefix-glob:PCommand/working-copy path contextPrefix-oriented globYou want recursive matching below a named prefix.
root-file:PRepository rootOne literal pathPortable scripts and docs.
root-glob:PRepository rootGlob patternPortable recursive selection.
root-prefix-glob:PRepository rootPrefix-oriented globPortable subtree selection.
none()—Empty setDisable a configured selection in an alias.
all()—All pathsExplicit whole-tree operation after inspection.

When a literal path contains a colon, use the appropriate quoting/escaping form from the installed help; a colon at the start of an atom is a language delimiter. When a shell variable supplies the expression, quote its expansion:

paths='root-glob:src/**/*.rs ~ root-glob:src/**/generated/**'
jj file list "$paths"
jj diff -r @ -- "$paths"

The second command illustrates a subtle boundary: a positional argument after -- is passed as one fileset to jj, but the command still decides whether that position means a fileset. Do not assume every command accepts the same separator convention; check its entry in Part XV.

ConsumerDefault tree/patchPartial selection effectPost-operation check
jj diffCurrent commit or endpointsLimits display, not graph.Compare with an unfiltered summary.
jj file listCurrent working tree/commitLimits listed paths.Run from root and subdirectory to test scope.
jj restoreFrom source to into destinationCopies selected paths.Diff the destination and inspect residual files.
jj squashFrom source change into destinationMoves selected patch paths.Diff both source and destination.
jj splitSelected commitAssigns selected paths/hunks to one side.Inspect both resulting commits.
jj fixSelected source commitsRestricts fixer input where supported.Query successors and run tests at the stack tip.
jj file chmodWorking-copy treeLimits executable-bit changes.Show file modes through Git/object inspection if relevant.
jj file track/untrackFilesystem tracking stateChanges what snapshot considers.Run status and inspect the next snapshot.

Appendix E — Template type and method map

Templates are easiest to look up by the value you currently have, not alphabetically by function name.

TypeImportant membersTypical renderingFailure to test
Commitcommit_id, change_id, description, parents, author, committer, state predicates, refs, diffLog/show records.Root, merge, conflict, empty, hidden, and divergent commits.
CommitIdshort, shortest, normal_hexHuman ID and exact object evidence.Ambiguous prefixes and new objects after rewrite.
ChangeIdshort, shortest, normal_hexLogical evolution identity.Divergent evolutions and changed IDs after explicit update.
Signaturename, email, timestampAuthor/committer fields.Different author/committer and timezone conversion.
Timestampago, format, utc, local, after, before, sinceReview age and reproducible dates.Locale, clock skew, and author versus committer time.
CommitRefname, remote, tracking/conflict/sync methods, targetsBookmark/tag/ref labels.Conflicted refs and removed/added targets.
RepoPath/FsPathabsolute, relative, display, parentPortable path output.Running from a subdirectory and sparse paths.
TreeDiffsummary, git, color_words, stat, filesPatch/stat output.Large diffs, binary files, conflict values.
TreeEntrypath/status/source/target/mode-like fieldsFile-level reports.Renames, deletes, copies, and executable bits.
Operationid, description, parents, time, user, snapshot/workspace fieldsOperation/recovery log.Unintegrated/concurrent operations.
WorkspaceRefname, target, rootWorkspace dashboards.Stale or moved workspace paths.
List/Optionmap, filter, join, first, last, try, coalesceConditional collections.Empty lists/options and wrong element type.

The same expression can have different meaning in different contexts. id in an operation template is an operation ID; commit_id in a commit template is a commit object ID. Prefer explicit field names in mixed documentation.

This task-oriented appendix is intentionally more useful than an alphabetic dump. For migration, generate and diff the 0.44 and 0.45 schemas, then review every changed key’s scope and default. Preserve a copy of the old configuration while testing.

Appendix F — Complete configuration families

Namespace/familyImportant members and design roleScope/edge case
user.*name, email; signing identity inputs.User scope normally; author can be overridden explicitly.
ui.*pager, colour, editor, diff/merge editor, default command, movement, timestamps, conflict markers, output style.Workspace/repo overrides can surprise prompts and automation.
git.*backend, push/fetch defaults, private commits, remote/ref behaviour.Push remote defaults must not silently choose GitHub versus Gerrit.
gerrit.*default-remote, default-remote-branch, review-url in documented newer surface.Use explicit 0.44 upload flags where a key is absent; 0.45.1 documents these keys.
revsets.*log, status, fix, sign, arrange, simplify-parents, converge/relevant defaults.Default selection is policy; inspect before mutation.
revset-aliasesNamed and parameterised commit sets.Aliases are parsed expressions and can shadow builtins.
fileset-aliasesNamed path sets and path-pattern functions.Separate namespace from revset aliases.
templates.*log, show, evolog, file, operation, workspace defaults; builtin templates.Human output defaults should not be assumed in scripts.
template-aliasesKeywords/functions/patterns for rendering.Builtin shadowing and optional values are common traps.
merge-tools.*External merge program, arguments, capabilities, conflict handling.External executable and exit status are security/automation concerns.
diff-editor/diff-tools.*Diff editing and rendering programs.Different from a merge editor; fileset and endpoint still come from command.
fix.tools.*Fixer command, file patterns, range support, environment.Can rewrite many commits; test line-range behaviour.
signing.*Backend, key, command/agent and verification policy.Sign/unsign rewrite commits; hardware signing can be slow.
immutable-head policyRevset defining rewrite protection.Changing config changes safety boundary; --ignore-immutable remains exceptional.
snapshot/workspaceSnapshot intervals, file tracking/ignore/size, sparse/workspace behaviour.Filesystem state and commit trees remain distinct.
command aliasesSafe shortcuts and publication commands.Document mutation, remote, and argument forwarding.

For an exhaustive machine check, compare jj util config-schema output between binaries. For a useful human configuration, explain why a key exists, where it is safe, and which other keys it interacts with; a list of key names alone is not a configuration reference.

Appendices H–K — operational recipes and Linux integration

The preceding reference cards describe the language and command surfaces. These appendices turn that information into short, repeatable operating procedures. Each recipe starts by naming the state that must be true before the command is run, and ends with an inspection step. That discipline matters in jj because the most dangerous errors are usually selection errors: the command is valid, but its commit set, destination, remote, or workspace is not the one the operator intended.

Appendix H — GitHub recipes fieldbook

GitHub consumes Git refs. jj supplies those refs through bookmarks and the Git transport, but the local graph does not need to be organised around them. In the recipes below, the local change IDs are the working identities and the bookmarks are publication handles. Replace origin explicitly when the repository has more than one remote.

H.1 Publish one mutable change as a PR

  1. Fetch the base and inspect it: jj git fetch --remote origin, then jj log -r 'trunk() | @ | ancestors(@)'.
  2. Put the working-copy commit on top of the fetched base if necessary. A publication bookmark is not required while the change is still private.
  3. Give the change a reviewable description and run tests at the intended commit. Record the exact commit with jj log -r @ -T 'change_id ++ " " ++ commit_id ++ "\n"'.
  4. Create a publication handle only when the hosting service needs a branch: jj bookmark create feature/allocator -r @.
  5. Push that handle to the intended remote: jj git push --remote origin --bookmark feature/allocator. The push result, not the mere existence of a local bookmark, is the publication event.
  6. Create or update the PR with the host tool. Verify the remote bookmark and local target before every update.
jj log -r 'feature/allocator | feature/allocator@origin'
jj bookmark list feature/allocator
jj git push --remote origin --bookmark feature/allocator

After review, edit or squash the local change. The resulting commit ID may change while the jj change ID remains the same. Move the bookmark to the successor only after checking jj diff -r 'feature/allocator@origin..@' and the test result. A rejected non-fast-forward push is a state disagreement to investigate, not a prompt to use an unqualified force operation.

H.2 Publish a stack of dependent PRs

Suppose the local graph is:

main@origin
    |
    A  (base)
    |
    B  feature/api
    |
    C  feature/cli
    |
    @  (scratch edits)

Make the publication boundary explicit. The first PR points at B and the second at C. Use distinct bookmarks, for example pr/api and pr/cli. Keep the lower bookmark at B while C is being reviewed; changing C must not silently publish B’s private edits.

jj bookmark create pr/api -r B
jj bookmark create pr/cli -r C
jj git push --remote origin --bookmark pr/api --bookmark pr/cli

When B changes, jj rebases its descendants as part of the rewrite. Inspect the successor graph before pushing both handles:

jj log -r 'ancestors(C) | descendants(B)'
jj diff -r 'pr/api@origin..B'
jj diff -r 'pr/cli@origin..C'
jj git push --remote origin --bookmark pr/api --bookmark pr/cli

The host may display a stack as separate PRs, a single stacked series, or a sequence of branches. That presentation is host policy. The local invariant is simpler: each publication bookmark names one graph node, and the upper node depends on the lower node.

H.3 Rebase a stack after main moves

Fetch first, then identify the first local commit that is not part of the new trunk. Do not blindly rebase every visible head: that may include unrelated workspaces or another feature.

jj git fetch --remote origin
jj log -r 'trunk()..pr/cli'
jj rebase -s 'pr/api' -d 'main@origin'
jj log -r 'main@origin..pr/cli'

If the stack has a lower public bookmark and an upper public bookmark, a source selection rooted at the lower change moves its descendants together. If you intentionally want only one change, select that commit and inspect the resulting descendants. Conflicts remain values in the graph; they do not imply a Git-style continuation state. Resolve them, test, and then push each affected bookmark.

H.4 Diagnose a rejected update

Classify the rejection before changing anything. Compare the local bookmark, the remote bookmark, and the graph between them:

jj bookmark list pr/api
jj log -r 'pr/api | pr/api@origin | ancestors(pr/api@origin)'
jj diff -r 'pr/api@origin..pr/api'
jj git push --remote origin --bookmark pr/api --dry-run

Common causes are a stale fetch, another contributor moving the branch, a host-side merge, or a bookmark whose target is no longer the intended successor. Fetch again and decide whether to integrate the remote tip, replace the remote branch through the project’s accepted force-update policy, or create a new publication bookmark. Keep the old remote target in the operation record and review notes. Never encode a force-push policy in a general-purpose alias without an explicit remote and bookmark.

H.5 Clean up after merge or closure

After GitHub merges a PR, fetch the remote and verify that the remote bookmark has been deleted or moved according to the repository policy. Delete or forget the local publication handle only after the remote state is understood:

jj git fetch --remote origin
jj bookmark list --remote origin
jj bookmark delete pr/api
jj log -r 'immutable_heads() | trunk()'

Deleting a local bookmark is not the same operation as deleting its remote counterpart. Remote deletion requires the Git push mode supported by the installed version and the project policy; use the explicit deleted-bookmark mechanism only after confirming its target. The commit remains reachable through other descendants, the operation log, or the remote object store until normal garbage collection makes it unreachable.

H.6 Use an upstream repository and a fork

Give the two remotes different roles. Fetch both, but publish only to the fork unless project policy explicitly permits publishing upstream. A local bookmark can be tracked against one remote bookmark at a time; the names can be identical while the remote relationship is different.

jj git remote add upstream https://github.com/example/project.git
jj git fetch --remote upstream
jj bookmark create fork/allocator -r @
jj git push --remote origin --bookmark fork/allocator

Use fully qualified remote bookmarks in revsets when the same branch name exists on both remotes. The name main@origin and main@upstream are different graph references even if they currently identify the same commit.

H.7 Combine jj with gh in a colocated repository

First make jj import any Git-side ref changes, then use gh for host operations that are not version-control graph operations. A PR merge performed by gh changes the remote; it does not directly update the local jj view until a fetch/import operation.

jj git fetch --remote origin
gh pr view --web
gh pr checks
jj git fetch --remote origin
jj log -r 'main@origin'

When invoking native Git plumbing, use GIT_DIR=$(jj git root) only after checking whether the repository is colocated and what the command will modify. Prefer read-only commands for diagnosis. If Git moves a ref, follow it with jj git fetch or the documented import operation before reasoning from jj’s remote-bookmark symbols.

Appendix I — Gerrit recipes fieldbook

Gerrit adds a server-side review identity to the Git transport. A jj change ID is generated by jj and follows an evolution in the local commit graph. Gerrit’s Change-Id is a commit-message footer interpreted by Gerrit to associate patch sets with a review. They are independent identifiers and should be displayed separately in review tooling.

I.1 Create and upload a patch stack

jj git fetch --remote origin
jj new main@origin
jj describe -m 'parser: accept quoted allocator names'
# edit files and let jj snapshot them
jj new
jj describe -m 'cli: expose allocator diagnostics'
jj log -r 'main@origin..@'
jj gerrit upload --remote origin --remote-branch main -r 'main@origin..@'

The exact upload selection and server defaults are release-sensitive. On 0.44, prefer explicit --remote and --remote-branch when a repository has GitHub and Gerrit remotes. On 0.45.x, inspect the Gerrit defaults before relying on them. Before upload, inspect each commit message for an existing Gerrit footer and decide whether the project’s hook or server tooling should add it. Do not assume jj’s change ID will satisfy Gerrit’s footer requirement.

I.2 Revise the bottom change after review

jj edit 'description(exact:"parser: accept quoted allocator names")'
# edit the parser and run focused tests
jj diff
jj log -r 'ancestors(@) | descendants(@)'
jj gerrit upload --remote origin --remote-branch main -r 'main@origin..@'

Editing an ancestor changes its commit object and normally rebases its descendants. The jj change ID of the edited change is the useful local continuity signal; the Gerrit footer is the server continuity signal. The descendants receive new commit IDs because their parent changed, even where their patches are otherwise unchanged. If the server rejects a patch set, preserve the local graph and inspect the generated commit messages rather than manually copying identifiers between unrelated changes.

I.3 Resolve a conflict in the middle of a stack

jj log -r 'main@origin..@'
jj resolve --list
jj resolve --tool :builtin -r @
# inspect each side, edit the materialised conflict, then snapshot
jj diff
jj status
jj gerrit upload --remote origin --remote-branch main -r 'main@origin..@'

A conflict in a lower stack member can make later members conflicted through rebasing. Resolve at the earliest useful node, then inspect descendants. A clean lower node does not prove that all descendants are clean: their patches may conflict independently. Upload only after querying conflicts() over the exact review range.

I.4 Continue after one change lands

Once Gerrit lands the lower review, fetch the new target and rebase the remaining local stack onto it. The old remote review bookmark may disappear or move according to Gerrit’s ref policy; use the fetched refs and commit messages to identify the remaining work.

jj git fetch --remote origin
jj log -r 'main@origin..@'
jj rebase -s 'description(glob:"cli: expose allocator diagnostics")' -d main@origin
jj log -r 'main@origin..@'
jj gerrit upload --remote origin --remote-branch main -r 'main@origin..@'

Do not re-upload a landed change merely because its old local bookmark remains. Decide whether the local commit is now an ancestor of the fetched trunk; if it is, delete or move the local publication handle. If it is not, investigate whether the server rebased, squashed, or rejected a related change.

I.5 Use upload options without hiding the publication boundary

Options such as reviewers, CCs, topic, hashtags, WIP/ready state, comments, and submit behaviour belong to the Gerrit upload operation. Keep them explicit in a reviewed command or a narrowly scoped alias. A command alias that silently supplies --submit, an alternate remote, or a broad commit set makes recovery harder and can publish unrelated work.

jj gerrit upload --remote origin --remote-branch main \
  --reviewer reviewer@example.org --topic allocator-stack \
  -r 'main@origin..@'

Because Gerrit server configuration and plugins vary, treat server-side topic interpretation, automatic footer insertion, submit queues, and policy checks as externally validated behaviour. The local jj invariants—commit selection, commit rewriting, conflicts, bookmarks, and operation recovery—can still be tested in a local Git repository.

I.6 Abandon or replace a review

Abandoning a Gerrit review does not abandon the local jj change. Keep the local node if its patch may be useful; create a new change description or a replacement branch only when the server workflow requires a new review identity. Record the old Gerrit footer in the review note and avoid carrying it to a deliberately unrelated change. If the local change was accidentally abandoned, recover it from jj op log, an evolution query, or another reachable descendant before publishing again.

Appendix J — Recovery recipes fieldbook

Recovery starts with observation. Stop making ordinary operations once the mistake is recognised, save the current operation ID, and inspect both graphs:

jj op log --limit 12
jj op show --operation @
jj log -r 'all()' --limit 80
jj evolog -r @

The commit DAG answers “what commits exist and how are they related?” The operation DAG answers “which repository view produced the current state, and what earlier views are available?” A bookmark may be absent from the current view while its target still exists in the commit store or an older operation view.

SymptomFirst inspectionPreferred recovery directionDo not assume
Wrong change editedjj op log, current/previous jj op showjj undo if the last operation is isolated; otherwise restore a named prior view.Commit ID alone identifies the intended operation.
Useful work abandonedjj evolog, jj log -r all(), op historyFind the abandoned successor and create a new visible/bookmarked name.Abandon immediately deletes the object.
Bookmark moved to wrong nodejj bookmark list, op diffMove it back explicitly after checking the intended target.Moving a bookmark rewrites commits.
Several operations occurred after mistakeOperation IDs and operation parentsRestore or inspect a prior operation; copy useful work forward deliberately.Repeated undo is always equivalent to restoring one known view.
Remote was changed incorrectlyLocal/remote bookmark targets plus server auditFetch, preserve evidence, then perform an approved ref correction.The local operation log can undo a server-side push.
Concurrent operation integrated unexpectedlyOperation log and operation descriptionsInspect the integrated view; recover a prior local view if needed.Operation IDs are Git reflog entries.

J.1 Recover the last mistaken rewrite

jj op log --limit 5
jj op show --operation @
jj undo
jj status
jj log -r 'heads(all())'

Use undo when the last operation is the mistake and no valuable operation should be preserved from that view. Confirm the resulting working-copy snapshot and bookmark targets. If the mistaken operation was followed by useful work, do not blindly undo a sequence; inspect the operation DAG and restore or reproduce the useful work in a controlled view.

J.2 Recover one known prior repository view

jj op log
jj op show --operation OPERATION_ID
jj op diff --from OPERATION_ID --to @
jj op restore --operation OPERATION_ID

Substitute an actual operation ID, not a commit ID. Operation restore changes the repository view, including refs and workspace state represented by that operation. It is therefore more powerful than moving a bookmark back. Immediately make a safety bookmark or export any work that should survive before continuing ordinary development.

J.3 Recover after a bad push

Local recovery and remote recovery are separate. First preserve the local state and identify the old remote target from logs, host audit, or another clone. Then contact the project owner if a protected ref or review ref was affected. A safe local sequence is:

jj bookmark list
jj op log
jj git fetch --remote origin
jj log -r 'all() & remote_bookmarks(remote=origin)'
# create a temporary local name before any correction
jj bookmark create recovery/bad-push -r 'pr/api@origin'

Only after the target is preserved should an approved push repair the remote bookmark. The operation log records the local push command, but it cannot rewind a remote server. This distinction is the operational boundary between jj recovery and Git-host administration.

Appendix K — Linux shell integration fieldbook

Shell integration should expose state without turning every prompt into a repository scan. Keep prompt commands read-only, cheap, and tolerant of directories outside a repository. Put mutation in explicit aliases or functions that print their selected commit and remote before acting.

K.1 A conservative Bash/Zsh prompt component

jj_prompt() {
  command -v jj >/dev/null 2>&1 || return 0
  jj root >/dev/null 2>&1 || return 0
  local state
  state=$(jj log --no-graph -r @ -T 'change_id.short() ++ " " ++ bookmarks' 2>/dev/null) || return 0
  printf '[jj:%s]' "$state"
}
PS1='\u@\h:\w $(jj_prompt)\$ '

Use command substitution carefully: a prompt must not invoke a command that snapshots, edits, pages, or contacts a remote. If the repository is large, replace the log query with a cached or less frequent hook. The prompt output is advisory; before publication, run an explicit status/log query in the terminal.

K.2 Safe inspection and publication functions

jj_head() {
  jj log --no-graph -r "$1" -T 'change_id ++ " " ++ commit_id ++ " " ++ description.first_line() ++ "\n'
}

jj_publish() {
  test "$#" -eq 2 || { echo 'usage: jj_publish REMOTE BOOKMARK' >&2; return 2; }
  local remote=$1 bookmark=$2
  jj bookmark list "$bookmark" || return
  jj log -r "$bookmark" -T 'bookmark ++ " => " ++ commit_id ++ "\n' || return
  jj git push --remote "$remote" --bookmark "$bookmark"
}

These functions deliberately take the remote and bookmark as arguments. A shell alias such as jj git push with no remote is convenient for a single-remote repository, but is a poor default for a working copy that can publish to both GitHub and Gerrit.

K.3 Pagers, editors, and external tools

IntegrationDiagnosticDesign rule
Pagerjj --no-pager logMake scripts independent of paging; configure human paging separately.
Colourjj --color never logMachine captures should disable colour and labels that are not data.
Editorjj config get ui.editorTest the executable and exit status before using it in a mutation alias.
Diff editorjj config get ui.diff-editorConfirm whether the tool edits a patch or only renders one.
Merge editorjj config get ui.merge-editorTest two-sided and multi-sided conflicts; do not assume Git’s marker layout.
SSH/GPG signingjj config list with secrets redactedValidate keys, agents, and verifier policy outside the repository fixture.
Completionjj util completion --helpInstall generated completion for the pinned binary; regenerate on upgrade.

K.4 Install completion without coupling scripts to a release

Generate completion from the binary that the shell will actually execute, then install it through the distribution’s supported mechanism. Keep the generated file outside the book repository unless it is intentionally versioned:

jj util completion bash > /tmp/jj-completion.bash
# inspect it before installing through the shell’s completion directory
jj util completion zsh > /tmp/_jj
jj util completion fish > /tmp/jj.fish

Completion is a convenience layer, not a compatibility contract. A 0.45 completion file can expose commands and options absent from 0.44. Pin the binary and completion together when reproducing a build or documenting a deployment image.

K.5 Automation boundary checklist

  1. Set JJ_BIN or call the pinned binary explicitly in CI.
  2. Use --no-pager and --color never for captured output.
  3. Pass commit and remote arguments explicitly; do not depend on a developer’s aliases.
  4. Use a machine-readable template only after testing empty, root, merge, conflict, and divergent cases.
  5. Capture the operation ID and command output for mutating jobs.
  6. Run the command in a disposable clone or workspace when the selection could rewrite multiple commits.
  7. After the command, query the intended invariant—bookmark target, conflict count, test result, or exact object ID.

This checklist is the Linux equivalent of a transaction boundary. jj makes rewriting routine, which is powerful for interactive work; automation should make the chosen scope and resulting graph equally explicit.

  • research/version-matrix.md — 0.44 baseline and 0.45.x comparison policy;

  • research/sources.md — official URLs, page titles, and source trail;
  • scripts/inventory-cli.sh — machine-generated command/help inventory;

  • research/completeness-report.md — generated after the validation run.

This HTML is authored as HTML rather than produced by converting Markdown. The research ledger and raw CLI captures are deliberately plain text so a future release comparison can be diffed, regenerated, and reviewed.
